- [surplus] UD3097 `tui/apps/install/disk-plan/src/presentation/use-recpart.ts:6-9` has two stacked doc comments for `useRecpartRun`. The first ("parsed once per run, so it keeps its identity") no longer belongs to anything; the second describes what the function does now.

### Leave as is

65 problems (1 parity, 39 special, 17 hidden, 5 needless, 3 surplus). Each with the designer's reason.

- [special] BC1129 `distro/builder/install-split-pane/src/main.rs:89-97` everything after `--left` belongs to the left pane until `--right`, and everything after `--right` to the right pane, so `--smoke` must precede `--left`; `distro/builder/guest/install-ux/install-ux.sh:18` and `:28` follow that order implicitly.
  - **Why left:** "Everything after `--left` until `--right` is the left argv" is the grammar that lets pane argv hold any word; it is in the usage text. (builder-core)
- [hidden] BC1132 `distro/builder/install-split-pane/src/main.rs:206` forces `TERM=xterm-256color` for both panes, a terminal model the hub and docs TUI apps rely on and the pane's own vt100 emulator must keep matching, with `key_bytes`/`control_byte` hand-encoding xterm sequences.
  - **Why left:** `TERM=xterm-256color` is what the pane's vt100/xterm emulator implements; the children must be told that model. (builder-core)
- [special] BC1135 `distro/builder/guest/install-ux/install-ux.sh:5` hard-codes `HOME=/root USER=root LOGNAME=root` for the install screen on both distros, independent of the user getty actually runs it as.
  - **Why left:** The live install screen runs as root on both distros by design (root autologin); the same on both siblings. (builder-core)
- [needless] BC1159 `distro/builder/src/install_ux.rs:65` resolves TUI apps with `distro_contract::tui_apps::find(repo_root, name)` from the repo's `tui/` JavaScript workspace at product-build time, so an ISO build depends on the TUI source tree's `package.json` manifests.
  - **Why left:** The ISO carries TUI apps built from the `tui/` workspace; reading their manifests at product build is the real producer relationship (BC-12 makes it reproducible). (builder-core)
- [hidden] BC2013 `distro/builder/install-split-pane/src/main.rs:43-44` the `HOST_KEYS` comment states facts owned by other programs' keymaps (Ctrl-] is free in bash, less and the docs app; Ctrl-G is less's file info). Nothing ties the docs app's keymap in `tui/` to this choice.
  - **Why left:** The `HOST_KEYS` comment records real key constraints of the programs shown; binding the docs app keymap to it costs more than the comment. (builder-core)
- [hidden] BC2015 `distro/builder/install-split-pane/src/lib.rs:3-4` the doc comment names its consumer (`cargo xtask check tui`), a fact xtask owns that goes stale when xtask changes.
  - **Why left:** Comment-only and true today (xtask uses `docs_pane_size`); Comments were not chosen as an addition. (builder-core)
- [special] BC2018 `distro/builder/guest/install-ux/install-ux.sh:6` `cd "$HOME" 2>/dev/null || cd /` swallows a missing-home error and starts the install screen in `/`.
  - **Why left:** Falling back to `/` keeps the getty-started screen always reaching a shell, which is the requirement. (builder-core)
- [special] BC3044 `distro/builder/src/process.rs:139` and `:178` `Cmd` spawns with the caller's full ambient environment (no `env_clear` or allowlist), so stray shell variables can steer the live callers `mkfs.erofs` (`artifact/rootfs.rs:117`) and `which` (`process.rs:287`) with no sign of it at the call.
  - **Why left:** Inheriting the caller's environment (PATH, TMPDIR, SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH) is needed by the spawned tools; an allowlist would break more than it protects. (builder-core)
- [special] BP106 `distro/builder/install-split-pane/src/main.rs:37,371-379` The smoke test has a fixed 8 s deadline and a 100 ms polling loop, and the live UI polls every 30 ms (`:41`).
  - **Why left:** The smoke's bounded 8 s wait on an observed marker and the UI's 30 ms input wait are the right shape for a TUI; event-driven redesign costs more than it saves. (builder-core)
- [special] BR020 `distro/builder/recipes/alpine-live-source-rootfs.rhai:250` runs `apk.static` as hard-coded `uid: 1000, gid: 1000`. That assumes one user id on the build host or worker.
  - **Why left:** apk.static usermode needs a non-root uid inside the Recipe worker; the uid layout is the worker's (tools/recipe/ginux, out of scope). (builder-recipes-guest)
- [special] BR046 `distro/builder/recipes/installed-system.rhai:73` keeps a hand list of BusyBox applets that must link to `bbsuid` (`mount`, `su`, `passwd`, ...), a copy of Alpine's busybox-suid packaging knowledge.
  - **Why left:** the bbsuid applet list lives in busybox-suid's install script, which `--no-scripts` skips; copying it is the price of not running scripts. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [special] BR061 `distro/builder/recipes/linux.rhai:9` and `linux-prebuilt.rhai:9` read the `KERNEL_TARGET` define with no default, so the public kernel recipes cannot run without a required define (AGENTS.md: "Normal public recipes run without required defines"). Four Rust call sites have to pass it (`src/recipe/linux.rs:62`, `src/pipeline/kernel.rs:54`, `xtask/src/tasks/kernels/common.rs:149`, `:200`).
  - **Why left:** `KERNEL_TARGET` is a real selector and Recipe's out-of-scope e2e suite drives the recipes with it; a default would be a new special case. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [special] BR070 `distro/builder/recipes/linux.rhai:34-90` `_kernel_make_env_prefix` derives `BISON_PKGDATADIR`, `M4`, `C_INCLUDE_PATH`, `LIBRARY_PATH` and `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` from wherever `bison` is on PATH, to suit both host tools and `linux-deps` RPM extraction. These are workarounds for one dependency source.
  - **Why left:** bison/M4 environment derivation compensates for extracted tools in the Recipe worker image (out of scope). (builder-recipes-guest)
- [special] BR071 `distro/builder/recipes/linux-deps.rhai:66-72` deletes the RPM-extracted `ld`, `ld.bfd` and `ld.gold` because they don't find `.tools/usr/lib64`, a workaround for one host/tool combination.
  - **Why left:** the extracted `ld` removal is the same worker-image workaround; host `ld` is used by design. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [hidden] BR073 `distro/builder/recipes/linux.rhai:5` `build_deps = ["linux-deps"]` names the dep recipe by bare name, resolved through `--recipes-path`. Every caller has to pass `distro/builder/recipes` as the recipes path for the source kernel to build.
  - **Why left:** `build_deps` by bare name is Recipe's API. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [special] BR074 `distro/builder/recipes/linux.rhai:24-28` embeds an `AUTOFIX_PROMPT` block for the Recipe engine's LLM autofix mode, which `xtask/src/tasks/kernels/common.rs:170-177` enables with `--autofix-allow-path distro/builder/recipes`. The kernel recipe's text is coupled to one xtask workflow's agent instructions.
  - **Why left:** `AUTOFIX_PROMPT` is how Recipe's autofix mode reads instructions; moving it needs a Recipe change. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [needless] BR096 `tools/recipe/tests/e2e/preseed_recipes.rs:22`, `:37` and `:81` (the out-of-scope Recipe engine's tests) read `distro/builder/recipes/sources/alpine.rhai` and `levitate-rpm-lock.rhai`. The engine's test suite depends on this area's pin files, so re-pinning a distro touches the engine's tests.
  - **Why left:** The Recipe engine's own e2e tests read this area's pin files. Changing that means editing `tools/recipe`, which is out of scope. Re-pinning touches those tests until Recipe's owner decouples them. (builder-cli)
- [special] BR104 `distro/builder/recipes/install-tools.rhai:79-81` sets RUSTFLAGS per target: a hard-coded `rust-lld` path under `/usr/lib/rustlib/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` for musl, plus `--threads=1` for both. These are environment-specific linker workarounds inside the tool recipe.
  - **Why left:** the `rust-lld` path is a fact of the Recipe compiler image (out of scope); the GNU half goes with RG-13. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [special] P052 `testing/install-tests/src/scenarios/ab_update.rs:269-271` stages LevitateOS's slot with `--initrd /boot/initramfs.img` (`INITRAMFS_LIVE_ISO_PATH`, the live initramfs), which `recsetup` refuses as unbootable for an installed system (`plan.rs:440-452`) and which the user docs replace with `initramfs-installed.img` (`07-post-installation.ts:69`); the A/B scenario tests a different update than users run, on LevitateOS only.
  - **Why left:** already fixed in current code. `ab_update.rs` stages with `boot/initramfs-installed.img`, the installed initramfs. The remaining literal is TI040 (TV-06). (testing-vm)
- [special] RG1100 `distro/builder/guest/install-ux/install-ux.sh:21-26`: the serial console is special-cased by matching `tty` against `/dev/ttyS*`, separately from the `consoles` list that decided getty starts the launcher there at all (`distro/builder/recipes/live-overlay.rhai:29-31,47-51`).
  - **Why left:** the consoles list decides where the launcher runs; the tty test decides the serial opt-in — two different facts. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [special] RG1133 `distro/builder/recipes/qemu-tools.rhai:87-94`: the iPXE ROMs are linked into `usr/share/qemu-kvm` by hand, standing in for links the packages' install scripts would otherwise have created.
  - **Why left:** iPXE ROM links are the only package-script effect the QEMU consumer needs; reproducing it in place is cheaper than a framework. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [special] RG2041 `distro/builder/recipes/installed-system.rhai:84` `if exists(dest) { continue; }` skips any BusyBox applet path that something already occupies, and keeps no record of which applets were not linked.
  - **Why left:** skipping occupied applet paths is the intended rule (full-size tools win over BusyBox). (builder-recipes-guest)
- [special] RG2086 `distro/builder/guest/live/shell-ux.sh:16,31-32` `eval "$(dircolors -b 2>/dev/null || true)"` and `shopt ... 2>/dev/null || true` swallow errors in a profile that every live login sources.
  - **Why left:** a login profile must never abort a login; swallowing `dircolors`/`shopt` errors is correct there. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [special] RG2093 `distro/builder/guest/install-ux/install-ux.sh:28-30` when the split pane exits with an error, it prints one fixed line and falls through to a root shell (degrade-and-continue). Nothing keeps a record of the failure for the user or the test harness.
  - **Why left:** falling back to a root shell is the intended install-screen UX; the user already sees the message. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [surplus] RG2115 `distro/builder/recipes/linux.rhai:194-198` and `linux-prebuilt.rhai:161-165` accept a `kernel_archive` input that no product caller supplies (`distro/builder/src/recipe/linux.rs`, `src/pipeline/kernel.rs`, `xtask/src/tasks/kernels/common.rs` pass none). Only the Recipe engine's own test `tools/recipe/tests/e2e/kernel_acquisition.rs:76` passes it.
  - **Why left:** the unused `kernel_archive` input is exercised by Recipe's own e2e suite (out of scope); removing it breaks that suite. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [hidden] RG2130 `distro/builder/recipes/linux.rhai:411,431,443,451`, `linux-prebuilt.rhai:277,281`, `custom-source-rootfs.rhai:64` and the `cleanup` hooks of the other recipes each type Recipe's cleanup-reason vocabulary by hand (`"manual"`, `"auto.acquire.success"`, `"auto.acquire.failure"`, `"auto.build.failure"`). An engine enum is spelled out as strings in every recipe.
  - **Why left:** cleanup reasons are Recipe's string API; recipes cannot import an engine enum. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [hidden] RG2134 `tools/recipe/tests/e2e/kernel_recipes.rs:4-15` copies `linux.rhai`, `linux-prebuilt.rhai`, `linux-deps.rhai` and `kernel/metadata.rhai` out of `distro/builder/recipes` by relative path. The generic engine's tests depend on these product kernel recipes, so editing them can break Recipe's suite.
  - **Why left:** Recipe's tests copying product kernel recipes is a link from out-of-scope code; RG-10 keeps the interface they use. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [hidden] RG3019 `distro/builder/guest/installed/system-setup.sh:83` writes `127.0.1.1 <host>.localdomain <host>` into `/etc/hosts` with a hard-coded `localdomain` suffix. That hosts-file policy belongs to neither the variant contract nor recsetup's hostname check (`tools/recstrap/src/bin/recsetup/plan.rs:320-327`), and it is fixed inside the guest script for both siblings.
  - **Why left:** the `/etc/hosts` policy has one owner (the setup script) and is the same for both siblings. (builder-recipes-guest)
- [hidden] TI053 `testing/install-tests/src/scenarios/ab_update.rs:255` finds the update medium as `blkid -t TYPE=iso9660 -o device | head -n1`. `session::spawn` also attaches the boot-injection ISO as an extra CD to every VM (`qemu/session.rs:92-95`, TI074), so the first iso9660 device depends on libvirt's SCSI unit order (media is pushed before injection only by call order in `spawn`).
  - **Why left:** already fixed in current code. `stage_from_media` selects the medium by `identity.iso_label`, not the first iso9660 device. (testing-vm)
- [special] TI084 `tools/recqemu/src/libvirt.rs:271-280` `wait_for_shutdown` polls `virsh domstate` every 500ms (a process spawn each time); `Console::connect` (`serial/console.rs:42-50`) polls the socket every 100ms.
  - **Why left:** a 500 ms `virsh domstate` poll and a 100 ms socket poll are cheap and bounded. An event API would cost more than it saves. (testing-vm)
- [special] TI088 `tools/recqemu/src/serial/boot.rs:37-40,52` `wait_for_boot` sends a newline every 3s once a line contains `"Linux version"` ("getty prompts on a headless serial console appear only after input"). It is a timed nudge keyed on one kernel banner string.
  - **Why left:** a headless getty only prints its prompt after input, so the timed newline is a real requirement. "Linux version" is the kernel's stable banner, not a distro fact. (testing-vm)
- [special] TI090 `tools/recqemu/src/serial/auth.rs:11-74` `login` drives getty by lowercase substrings `"login:"`, `"password"`, `"login incorrect"`, `"authentication failure"`, retries up to `MAX_ATTEMPTS = 3`, and re-types a probe whenever the console has been quiet for 1s.
  - **Why left:** driving an interactive getty is inherently prompt-text based. It is one function in one crate, and TV-14 uses it as the real console-login path users take. (testing-vm)
- [special] TI093 `tools/recqemu/src/serial/console.rs:108` `PARTIAL_LINE_DELAY` = 100ms decides when a prompt without a newline counts as a line, a timing guess that `login`'s prompt detection depends on.
  - **Why left:** deciding that a prompt without a newline is a line needs a timing threshold. One constant, in the one place that reads the console. (testing-vm)
- [special] TL047 `tools/recinit/templates/init_tiny.template:350-361` the production live init writes root's `authorized_keys` from the test injection payload, a test-only feature inside every shipped live initramfs.
  - **Why left:** F34 kept the boot-injection import in the live init as a named interface; the live system already autologins root on its console, so a key from an attached medium grants nothing physical access does not. (tools)
- [special] TL081 `tools/recab/src/lib.rs:323-327` failing to record a failed trial is only a warning, and `tools/recab/src/store.rs:145-151` audit log failures are warnings; swallowed errors by design.
  - **Why left:** During a failed trial rebooting into the known-good slot matters more than the audit record; a failed record write must not block the reboot. (tools)
- [needless] TL102 `tools/recinit/Cargo.toml:22` an on-target tool crate depends on `distro/elf` (`leviso-elf`, the old project name), so `tools/` depends on `distro/`.
  - **Why left:** distro/elf is a stable leaf with no tool vocabulary, so the edge passes test (c); moving or renaming it is the contract area's call. (tools)
- [special] TL108 `tools/recstrap/tests/recstrap.rs:119,153,187,211` four `#[ignore = "needs root..."]` tests, and `:22-24`, `:237-238`, `:279-287` tests that print "skipped" and return depending on the host.
  - **Why left:** Root-only destructive checks cannot run unprivileged; F31 chose to keep them ignored-and-reported rather than passing. (tools)
- [special] TL109 `tools/recstrap/tests/recfstab.rs:78` and `tools/recpart/tests/loop_disk.rs:58` ignored root-only tests; `tools/recstrap/tests/recchroot.rs:57-60` and `tools/recab/tests/lifecycle.rs:250-252` skip when run as root.
  - **Why left:** Same as TL108 (F31): root-gated tests skip and say so. (tools)
- [special] TO1056 `tools/recpart/src/exec.rs:453,577` waits a fixed 10 s for the kernel's partition list, polling every 250 ms.
  - **Why left:** Previous untangle B116: partition nodes appear asynchronously, so a bounded poll in one place is the right wait. (tools)
- [special] TO1058 `tools/recpart/src/exec.rs:101-102,166` `skip_disk_checks` is a parameter for the one caller that plans from a recorded inventory (`cli.rs:428`), threaded through `ApplyOptions` and back out as `disk_checks_skipped` (`cli.rs:445`).
  - **Why left:** Planning from a recorded inventory is a real mode (TUI fixtures, offline plans) and must skip live-disk checks; one typed flag is the cheapest honest form. (tools)
- [special] TO1060 `tools/recpart/src/exec.rs:316-318` omits `wipefs --force` on purpose so wipefs refuses busy devices: a safety check implemented by leaving a flag off another program.
  - **Why left:** Refusing busy devices is the wanted behaviour, and leaving `--force` off wipefs is how wipefs offers it; documented at the call. (tools)
- [special] TO1062 `tools/recpart/src/preflight.rs:414-425` `list-disks` filters out devices whose name starts with `zram`, a name-based special case for one device type.
  - **Why left:** zram devices are compressed swap, never install targets; one filter in the one disk inventory (previous R7). (tools)
- [special] TO1063 `tools/recpart/src/preflight.rs:42-57` `LsblkFlag` accepts both JSON booleans and `"0"`/`"1"` strings for util-linux before 2.37, a compatibility branch for older hosts.
  - **Why left:** Previous untangle B109: util-linux versions differ in lsblk's JSON encoding; the compatibility lives in the one parser. (tools)
- [special] TO1122 `tools/recinit/src/busybox.rs:15-21` runs the busybox input binary on the build host (`--list`) to learn its applets; a busybox that does not run on the build host's architecture or libc cannot be packaged.
  - **Why left:** Both distros build x86_64 on x86_64 hosts; F13 chose to ask busybox for its applets. Revisit only if a cross-architecture build is added. (tools)
- [special] TO2105 `tools/recstrap/src/bin/recstrap/guards.rs:162-174` the mount-point guard compares `st_dev` with the parent, the comparison `:144-151` rejects for the host-root guard because btrfs subvolumes give each subvolume its own `st_dev`; one file applies both rules.
  - **Why left:** The two guards ask different questions (is the target a mount point vs is it the host root); the btrfs st_dev rule matters only to the second. (tools)
- [hidden] TO2170 `tools/recuki/src/lib.rs:1-5` and `tools/reciso/src/lib.rs:309-326` the live medium boots UKIs built by host `ukify`, while installed systems boot type-1 entries with separate `vmlinuz`/`initramfs.img` (recab, recsetup): two boot formats, two command-line owners (UKI-embedded vs entry `options`), and Secure Boot reasoning in recuki's doc applies to the live medium only.
  - **Why left:** Live media need a menu of UKIs with embedded command lines and installed slots need per-slot kernel/initramfs dirs; the two formats are a real requirement. Installed UKIs and Secure Boot are a product decision for both siblings (contract area: `installed_uki`). (tools)
- [special] TS1022 `testing/ginux-boot/acorn/instance.toml:19` uses contract profile `gate9-systemd-v1` for an OpenRC/busybox boot; AcornOS is run under a systemd-named contract.
  - **Why left:** `gate9-systemd-v1` is Ginux's contract profile name, and Ginux (out of scope) offers no init-neutral one. Using it for AcornOS is Ginux's naming. (testing-static)
- [hidden] TS1026 `testing/ginux-boot/ginux-boot.sh:42-43` hard-codes `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` and the binary path `target/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/release/ginux`, mirroring Ginux's crate/bin naming (`-p ginux-init` builds bin `ginux`).
  - **Why left:** The `--target` triple and binary path are Ginux's build layout (out of scope). They are spelled once in the driver. (testing-static)
- [hidden] TS1034 `testing/ginux-boot/ginux-boot.sh:35` requires `/sbin/init` and `instance.toml` `init = "/sbin/init"` in both OSes; on LevitateOS `/sbin/init` is a symlink to systemd and on AcornOS to busybox, an assumption about the rootfs layout owned by the rootfs recipes.
  - **Why left:** `/sbin/init` is the init entry point both rootfs recipes provide (systemd vs busybox). It is a real shared contract, not an assumption. (testing-static)
- [special] TS1035 `testing/ginux-boot/levitate/ginux-probe.service:6-7` uses `Type=exec` with a comment that a oneshot would keep the system "starting"; the probe's unit type is shaped around `is-system-running --wait` in `probe.sh`.
  - **Why left:** `Type=exec` is required because the probe waits for startup to finish. A oneshot would deadlock that wait. The comment states the reason. (testing-static)
- [hidden] UD2012 `tui/apps/live-tools/install-docs/package.json:102` the `tabs in code` story expects recfstab's literal output line `UUID=ABCD-1234  /boot   vfat    relatime,fmask=0022,dmask=0022`, and `:116,:120` expect `ls /sys/firmware/efi/efivars` and `loadkeys us`. The viewer's stories pin docs-content page text and recfstab's option string, so a docs edit fails the TUI check.
  - **Why left:** install-docs' stories are end-to-end render checks of the shipped docs; asserting shipped text (including recfstab's documented line) is what they check, so a docs edit that changes them should be re-recorded. Tying them to generated ids would check less. (tui-docs)
- [special] UD2072 `tui/apps/install/disk-plan/src/domain/layout.ts:228` always sends `swap_file: null`. recpart's `ManualLayout` supports a swap file (`src/generated/recpart.ts:170`), but the editor cannot ask for one, so the install UI cannot reach that recpart capability.
  - **Why left:** the editor cannot ask for recpart's swap file. That is a missing feature, not a tangle (recpart's default layout has no swap either). Add it if a user needs it. (tui-docs)
- [hidden] UD2074 `tui/apps/install/disk-plan/package.json:208` (story `disk refused`) expects `Disk Preparation › 2. Identify Target Disk`, and `:107` expects `recstrap /mnt`. disk-plan's checks pin docs-content section titles (including their numbering) and the install target path.
  - **Why left:** disk-plan's `disk refused` story asserts the guide reference the user actually sees. After UD-05 the reference is typed by id, so a rename still fails the typecheck first and the story only re-records the visible title. (tui-docs)
- [special] X057 `xtask/src/tasks/kernels/common.rs:48-64` a time-of-day gate (23:00-10:00) on kernel compiles that shells out to `date`, and it reports policy as a "missing prerequisite" (exit 3). The window is restated in `cli/types.rs:268`, `common.rs:20`, `xtask/README.md:52` and `architecture.md:30`.
  - **Why left:** The 23:00-10:00 kernel compile window is the user's deliberate host policy, and `--now` overrides it. Its restatements are help text for that flag. It stays in xtask (front-door policy) when XT-06 moves the kernel build. (xtask)
- [needless] X094 `xtask/src/tasks/tui/check.rs:74` reaches into `install_split_pane::docs_pane_size` (a builder sub-crate) to get the pane geometry; xtask depends on the builder's layout code.
  - **Why left:** `install_split_pane::docs_pane_size` is the shipped geometry's owner and a dependency-free leaf crate. Using it is "use the existing owner". (xtask)
- [surplus] X108 `justfile:7-36` hand-written one-line aliases for a subset of xtask commands (no `env`, `base`, `reset`, `inspect`), each a pass-through that drifts when a command is added.
  - **Why left:** AGENTS.md keeps `just` as short aliases by choice. They are one-line pass-throughs, and drift costs little. (xtask)
- [hidden] X109 `justfile:38-40` `clean-out` deletes `.artifacts/out` by path, knowing the producers' output layout, and `justfile:42-46` website recipes bypass the "one front door" that `justfile:1-2` claims.
  - **Why left:** `clean-out` deletes `.artifacts/out`, which AGENTS.md names as the output root, a stable fact. The website recipes are docs-site conveniences, not workflows. (XT-13 moves inspect output out of that tree.) (xtask)
- [hidden] XT1023 `deny.toml:20-23` clarifies `ring`'s license against a fixed `LICENSE` file hash (`0xbd0eed23`). Any ring bump that touches its LICENSE breaks the license check, and nothing next to the dependency says so.
  - **Why left:** cargo-deny requires a LICENSE hash in a `clarify` entry. Breaking on a ring LICENSE change is the intended review point. (xtask)
- [special] XT1024 `deny.toml:32-33` turns off unmaintained-crate advisories entirely (`unmaintained = "none"`) and only warns on yanked crates, a blanket exemption instead of per-crate ignores. `deny.toml:26` likewise only warns on duplicate versions.
  - **Why left:** Advisory strictness is a policy choice for the repo owner, not a structural tangle. (xtask)
- [hidden] XT1083 `xtask/src/util/repo.rs:5-10` `repo_root` is the parent of the compile-time `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR`, so a copied or installed `levitate-xtask` binary (CI runs `target/release/levitate-xtask`, `ci.yml:65-80`) always acts on the checkout it was built from, not on the cwd.
  - **Why left:** `cargo xtask` is always built from, and run inside, the checkout it serves. CI's binary runs in the same checkout. (xtask)
- [special] XT1100 `xtask/src/tasks/scenarios.rs:454-487,627` `flush_tty_input_queue` (`tcflush` on `/dev/tty`, all errors ignored) works around a CSI 6n reply racing with SSH/virsh session teardown, and `scenarios.rs:465` forces `TERM=vt100` for the interactive SSH session.
  - **Why left:** The tcflush and `TERM=vt100` work around a real terminal race in `virsh console` and SSH teardown. They stay, in the one interactive path XT-04 keeps. (xtask)
- [hidden] XT1145 `Cargo.toml:3-22` the workspace member list overlaps the `.gitmodules` paths (each submodule crate is listed in both) and includes the out-of-scope `tools/recipe`. The two co-change in 9 commits (`map/cochange-super.txt`), so adding or removing a crate submodule takes edits in both.
  - **Why left:** Cargo requires the members listed explicitly, and `.gitmodules` requires its paths. The two lists serve different tools, and adding a crate submodule rarely happens. (xtask)
- [surplus] Z029 `testing/ginux-boot/ginux-boot.sh` has no caller: no xtask command, scenario, justfile alias or doc outside its README runs it; it needs a hand-checked-out Ginux branch (`levitate/boot`).
  - **Why left:** ginux-boot has no xtask caller on purpose. It is a manual Ginux development probe that needs sudo and an unmerged Ginux branch. Wire it into scenarios only once the Ginux boot fixes are upstream. (testing-static)
- [parity] Z030 `testing/ginux-boot/levitate/expect` lists 30 known gaps, `acorn/expect` 6; the two distros boot to different degrees under Ginux and the lists are maintained separately.
  - **Why left:** The LevitateOS and AcornOS gap lists differ in length because systemd hits more unimplemented Ginux syscalls than OpenRC. That is data, not design. After TS-05 they are judged by the same rule. (testing-static)
- [needless] Z034 `xtask` and `install-tests` both depend on almost every crate (`map/crates.txt`), and both orchestrate scenarios (`xtask/src/tasks/scenarios.rs`, `testing/install-tests/src/scenarios/mod.rs`; co-change 9 in the superproject).
  - **Why left:** the two-orchestrator overlap belongs to the xtask / testing-vm orchestrator fix; nothing in recipes or guest scripts changes for it. (builder-recipes-guest)

## Progress

Refactor started 2026-11-07. Per AGENTS.md there are no branches: each fix is committed straight to each repo's one branch (submodules first, then the superproject pointer), so each fix is one commit per repo it touches and can be reviewed or reverted on its own.

**Re-run baseline before the refactor** (superproject `37673d7`). `cargo xtask check` now passes: "generated files current" is fixed since the audit baseline. `cargo xtask check tui` fails 6 checks, all from other sessions' commits after the audit:
- disk-plan stories "apply succeeds", "apply fails", "apply refused: the disk changed", "disk refused" and "edit: dual boot by hand" fail (tui `28714e5` "disk-plan: a state machine").
- The install-docs snapshot differs (docs/content `8cda564` "Install guides: base-system for steps 6-8").

These are the reference failures for the final comparison (`.artifacts/untangle-repo/refactor/check*-pre.txt`).

- [x] G01 — Delete the pre-Recipe build engine. builder `edb30dc`, superproject commit "G01: …". Removed 4.5k lines: `alpine/`, `artifact_store/`, `artifact/disk/`, `artifact/iso.rs`, `build/`, `cache`, `component/`, `contracts/`, `executor/`, `preflight/`, `timing`, `atomic_move`, `recipe::clear_cache`; the `fs2`, `sha2`, `tar`, `zstd` and `dirs` dependencies and the crates.io metadata. `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` passes. Left for later fixes: the legacy `leviso` names in `pipeline/{plan,paths}.rs` (G06), the PATH fallback that uses `which` (G09), and the builder docs (G32).
- [x] G02 — Delete the checkers that nothing runs or that check one distro only. recinit `0579548`, builder `bfde30e`, fsdbg `866978b`, install-tests `ebb0f5f`, superproject commit "G02: …".
  - **Gone:** the `testing/hardware-compat`, `testing/rootfs-tests` and `distro/elf` submodules (from workspace members, `.gitmodules`, CI, README and AGENTS.md); `distro/builder/tests` and `custom-source-rootfs.rhai`, which only those tests used; and fsdbg's binary, CLI, rootfs/auth-audit/qcow2 checklists, `oracle/`, EROFS reader and format detection.
  - **What stays:** fsdbg keeps the CPIO/ISO readers and the three checklists preflight calls (until G26). The ELF dependency walker and library copy are now recinit's private `src/elf`.
  - **CI change:** CI loses the "Verify the composed product rootfs" step. It ran rootfs-tests on LevitateOS only, against a `run-*` glob that no release directory matches any more.
  - **Behaviour change:** the builder's `copy_dir_recursive` recreates a symlink to a directory as a symlink instead of copying the tree behind it (CV3118).
  - **Still to do:** archiving the GitHub repos `LevitateOS/hardware-compat`, `rootfs-tests` and `leviso-elf` is the user's step (decision D17). Their checkouts remain under `.git/modules`.
- [x] G03 — The contract is one typed file per variant. contract `1f89422`, builder `9f44ee1`, install-tests `093bc3d`, docs/content `901c580`, tui `a67a6ec`, superproject commit "G03: …".
  - **Variant layout:** each variant is `distro.toml` (all former identity/ring0–3/scenarios/build-host sections, shaped like `ConformanceContract`), `kconfig`, `keys/`, `hooks/` and a README; `_shared/hooks/`.
  - **Contract:** `distro_contract::load(repo_root, id)` reads, validates (one rule per value kind, one violation per bad value, with its field path) and derives `artifacts`; `distro_ids` lists the variants. `distro/contract/src` went from 5324 to 1701 lines.
  - **Gone:** the serde mirrors and converters, the legacy flat layout, `VariantOwnerPaths`, the upward repo-root search, the build-host manifest, the evidence scripts and their runner, `build-host/recipes/kernel.rhai`, the merged live-boot defaults (now written in each file), `ViolationCode`, and the anti-gaming and placeholder rules. The kernel layout constants are `distro_builder::kernel`.
  - **Verified:** a `{:#?}` dump of the loaded contract for both distros, taken before and after, differs only in `schema_version` 6→7, the removed build fields, and `signing_key` `ring3/keys/…` → `keys/…`. Invalid files (bad console name, absolute path, `..`, unknown field, duplicate app) each give one error. `cargo xtask check` passes. `check tui` gives the same failure as the re-run baseline: the install-docs snapshot. The five disk-plan stories now pass. An AcornOS live-tools ISO built through the new contract and hooks (`A1KLTLnNeq5gN1qS`).
  - **Behaviour change:** every caller now fails on an invalid variant; the load checks that recipe, input, kconfig and key paths exist and that output counts are exact. distro-builder takes the repo root from its working directory.
  - **Kept for later fixes:** `extends` and `installed_uki`, so the dump stays equivalent (dropping them is the D17 decision); the positional live/emergency/debug UKI order (G16); the `leviso`/`acornos` aliases (G06); the distro lists in xtask and install-tests (G05); the builder's migration docs (G32).
- [x] G04 — Each variant declares its distro profile once. contract `b6e5546`, builder `e068584`, recstrap `911a7a4`, recab `82bdb4d`, install-tests `81a3cd7`, tui `fe052ae`, docs/content `b08f830`, superproject commit "G04: …".
  - **New leaf crate `distro/profile`** (`distro-profile`): `PackageBackend`, `InitSystem`, `Libc` (and the Rust and bun targets that follow from it), `PrivilegeTool`, `Desktop`, `KernelLine`, `Profile`. Its `REC_*` os-release fields are written into each image's `/etc/os-release` by the base product, and `Profile::from_os_release` reads them back.
  - **Contract:** `[profile]` in `distro.toml`. The iso label, default hostname, login prompt and kernel localversion are derived from the id, with the same values. `OverlayKind` and the per-action `target` are gone.
  - **Readers stop probing and name-matching:**
    - the builder's live tools, tool targets and bun target;
    - `system-setup.sh`;
    - recsetup and recstrap (privilege tool, user tool, init, locale);
    - recab's init system;
    - install-tests, where `DistroContext` and `src/distro/{levitate,acorn}.rs` are replaced by the loaded variant;
    - xtask gen, which writes `docs/content/src/distros.ts` for the TUIs.
  - **Behaviour change:**
    - recsetup, recab and `system-setup.sh` refuse a target whose os-release lacks the `REC_*` fields.
    - recstrap validates an extracted tree by its profile instead of `ESSENTIAL_DIRS`.
    - New users get root's shell from the target's passwd.
    - Only compiled glibc or musl locales are offered (no `localedef`/`locale-gen` path; neither distro used one).
    - OpenRC keymaps go through `loadkmap` only.
    - `UTC` needs the zoneinfo file like any other zone.
  - **Bug fixed along the way:** turning the systemd probe in the release hook into the declared fact exposed that base-rootfs is not the whole system (G14). base-rootfs now explicitly doesn't carry the installed initramfs (`carries_installed_system`), as the probe had silently arranged.
  - **Verified:**
    - `cargo xtask check` passes; `check tui` shows only the known install-docs snapshot difference.
    - The contract dump differs only by `[profile]` and the removed `live_overlay.kind` and `target` fields.
    - base-rootfs, live-boot and live-tools ISOs build for both distros, and base-rootfs's `/etc/os-release` carries the profile (`REC_INIT=openrc`/`systemd`, …).
    - The live-boot/live-tools ISOs built in this run predate the base-rootfs rebuild, so they still carry the old os-release. They need rebuilding (deferred list below) before recsetup or recab can be tried in them.
  - **Left:** P009 and P022 (product decisions, G07); TL016, TO3145 and Z016 partly (keymap and service backends, G13); CL3153 (the recipe's `BIN` default stays: public recipes take no required defines); C084 (UKI/ISO names, G05). U011, U015, U037–U041, U080–U082, U092, U093, UD1032, UD1048 and UD1135 now have generated data and move to G22/G23.

**Deferred to a daytime batch (after 10:00; the build fans are too loud at night).** Run in one go, in this order:
1. Rebuild for both distros: `release build iso live-boot <distro>`, then `live-tools`. That's ~45 min through `/tmp/iso-run-fixed.sh target/debug/distro-builder …`.
2. Check `/etc/os-release` in each live rootfs for the `REC_*` lines (`dump.erofs --cat --path=/etc/os-release`).
3. Run the VM scenarios once G26 drops preflight's privileged Recipe kernel check. Until then, scenarios can't run in this environment: as vince, the check needs Ginux host privileges; as root, the libvirt session VM can't start.
- [x] G05 — One registry of products and scenarios, and scenario expectations derived from it. contract `3c6db7b`, builder `42ed5cc`, install-tests `d9724be`, tui `9f6bd73` (README line only; pointer not moved), superproject `4aec62e` "G05: …".
  - **Registry:** `distro_contract::registry` names the products (`ProductId`: name, title, parent, chain, `is_release`, the scenarios that take its ISO, `next_scenario`) and the scenarios (`ScenarioId`: key, title, summary, `input()` = ISO product and/or install run, `is_runnable`, `ALL` in `test all` order) once. xtask, the builder CLI and planner, and install-tests use them; `release_product`, `scenario_for`, `scenarios_for`, `require_inputs`' table, `TestScenario`, `RunScenario`, `Product` and the `PRODUCT_*` constants are gone.
  - **Contract (schema 8):** `distro.toml` loses `products`, `product_config` (`install_ux` is top-level), `transforms`, `release` and `scenarios`, with `extends` and `installed_uki`. What really differs is declared as `[live] services/kernel_cmdline/tools` and `[installed] initramfs/tools`, each holding only the variant's additions. Shared lists, init-system cmdline tokens and unit names (`LiveService::units(init)`) are code. `contract.artifacts` (`ArtifactNames`) is derived from `os_id`: the per-product ISO, the release ISO name, the live UKIs by role and the image names. `identity.login_prompt` is derived too. The validator rejects an addition that repeats a shared value.
  - **Builder:** `product.rs` is the one table of build properties keyed by `ProductId` (overlay dir, source pointer, `live_session`, `carries_installed_system`). `ReleaseProduct` carries the hook. The planner is the fixed parent chain: no graph walker, logical names, identity maps or `unreachable!`. Live tools and boot payload producers are builder code, and the unused producer kinds are deleted. Library errors name no CLI syntax.
  - **install-tests:** `expect.rs` derives boot patterns (a shared list plus per-init lists), evidence (`<key>.sh`, `<KEY> PASSED`) and the root password (the variant id). `run_scenario(id, force)` returns the failure with its cause. Status shows `ERROR` when inputs cannot be resolved. The fingerprint is built from the registry inputs plus test-scripts and `distro.toml` content. Failure hints are one exhaustive table. The install mode is a harness constant.
  - **xtask:** `cli.rs` replaces `cli/`. Distros are discovered (clap parser over `distro_ids`, loaded once), and the distro argument is required everywhere (`--all` where it applies). The build subcommands, the scenario lists and the `--help` loop come from the registry.
  - **Verified:** `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` passes. `cargo xtask check` fails only "generated files current" (recstrap.ts, recpart.ts, tool-help.ts), from other sessions' uncommitted recstrap/recpart edits. The help and status output lists all 8 scenarios for both distros. Bad `[live]`/`[installed]` values each give one violation.
    - Before/after listing of every derived name and expectation (scratchpad `names-before`/`names-after`): equal, except the base-rootfs ISO name, `installed_uki` gone, the runtime evidence script name, and acorn's dead `ERROR: cannot start` live pattern (`ERROR: ` already matched it).
    - Live cmdline tokens are the same; their order changed.
  - **Behaviour change:**
    - The base-rootfs ISO is `<os_id>-x86_64-base-rootfs.iso`.
    - `test all` runs all 8 scenarios and does not stop at a failure; it returns the first error and lists the rest.
    - The distro is required (xtask and `distro-builder release build iso`).
    - `installed-tools.sh` → `runtime.sh` (`RUNTIME PASSED`).
    - The installed-boot payload no longer gets `.live-payload-role` (C078).
    - Doctor also checks the base-rootfs ISO.
    - Every cached scenario result shows STALE once (new fingerprint format).
  - **Not run:** ISO builds, VMs and xtask's `tests/cli.rs` (updated for STALE-or-ERROR).
  - **Left:**
    - C100, C102, C103, TI124, CV2071: the per-init strictness differences are kept, including `"Welcome to <os_name>"` on openrc. They go to G26 (one oracle), G07 (auditd) and G28 (NetworkManager; P031).
    - C080 in part: install_ux consoles, apps and hub → G21.
    - TI021, TI029 (the preflight gate) → G26.
    - TV2136 (mutable branches, kept per D11) → G26.
    - TI028 (harness mtime in the fingerprint) → G06 run records.
    - X001, builder part (`normalize_distro_id` aliases) → G06.
- [x] G18 — The install tools are one family with shared target-side primitives, built one way. recstrap `fb86bee`, recab `947d985`, recpart `9340d7d`, recinit `03f3f5c`, reciso `1eff154`, builder `0c5ed00`, superproject commit "G18: …".
  - **Decision taken:** no repo merge. The shared layer is the new crate `target-root` at `tools/recstrap/target-root` (workspace member, depends on libc only). recpart, recab, recstrap and recinit depend on it by path; the builder's source closure picks it up as a path dependency (checked with `distro-builder artifact prepare-tool-source recab`).
  - **target-root owns:** the probe rule (only not-found means absent), `PROTECTED_PATHS`/`is_protected_path`/`is_root`, os-release under a root, account names/root shell/merge, command running (`run`, `status_text`), `blkid_tag`/`partuuid` (lower-case), `block_device_bytes`, the mountinfo table (`Mount` with fstype and findmnt-style options), `unescape_octal`, `mount_holding`, `mounted_at`, durable `write_durable`/`copy_durable`/`sync_dir`, and the EROFS image check (`.erofs` name for files + superblock).
  - **recab:** `config.rs` reads every `RECAB_*` override in one function; `Config::install_target(root)` replaces `at_boot_root`; efivars are plain files only when `RECAB_EFIVARS_PATH` is set. Every changing command checks root and takes the lock; `judge_trial` is the one verdict (startup, slots or none, firmware asked lazily); a failed trial rolls back under the same lock from the state it wrote; `plan_stage` has no `Option`; `Operation::Init`; stage reads the PARTUUID once; stage mounts under `config.stage_dir`. `install-unit --package <init>` writes the packaged service file into a package tree.
  - **recstrap family:** recfstab reads mountinfo (no findmnt), writes fstab durably keeping its mode, resolves the root once. Sessions make private dirs under `/run`, mount a block-device image without `loop`, and as root stop on a failed `unshare`. recsetup runs chroot steps in its own `Session` (`prepare_chroot`/`chroot_command`, shared with recchroot), fails a step whose child did not read its passwords, and reads the target fail-closed. `install_tools::target_system` is gone.
  - **recpart:** guard from target-root; in-use checks, the partition listing, wipefs/sfdisk JSON fail closed (wipefs absent/refused and sfdisk finding no table still fall back to lsblk); an invalid logical sector size is refused at the lsblk read and in a loaded inventory instead of `max(512)` in four places.
  - **recinit/reciso (TO-11):** removed the `modules` subcommand, `ModulePreset`, `build-install --modules`, `--template`, `--rootfs-path`, `--live-overlay-image-path`, `boot_devices`, `PRODUCT_TAG` (hook passes `--boot-banner` instead of `--set`); cpio refuses special files, >4 GiB files and non-UTF-8 names. reciso has only `UkiSpec` (built UKIs), no `--uki`, no fallback UKI; at least one `--build-uki` is required.
  - **Recipe (RG-13):** `install-tools.rhai` builds every tool `--target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl` (no GNU branch, native-host check, allowlist, `INIT_SYSTEM`/`RUST_TARGET` defaults); `--version` checked once after the build; recab packages its own trial service via `recab install-unit --package $INIT_SYSTEM`, so the recipe knows no service file, path or `trial_service` override.
  - **Verified:** `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` clean. Crate CLI tests pass: recab lifecycle 20/20 (fixtures adjusted: the fake `systemctl list-units` exits 0; the blocked-efivar fixture is a dangling symlink, since a directory now reads as an error), recstrap all, recpart all, recinit 2/2. Host musl build of recab/recpart/recstrap/recsetup/recchroot/recfstab/install-split gives static-pie binaries; `recab install-unit --package` writes the right file per init. recfstab's merged mountinfo options equal findmnt's OPTIONS for all 28 mounts on this host. Not run (quiet hours): ISO builds and VM scenarios; the recipe itself is unrun.
  - **Behaviour changes:** LevitateOS install tools are static musl (the decision). Non-root `recab stage`/`set-next`/`commit`/`rollback`/`trial` stop at the root check before reading anything. Probes that silently passed now stop with a named path (efivars, cmdline, entry files, OpenRC state, `/proc/swaps`, holders, account files, `/boot` stat, resolv.conf). recab stage refuses a non-`.erofs` image file up front (recstrap did later). recchroot: a DNS bind failure is an error; a cleanup failure no longer replaces the command's exit status. recfstab: the `# origin` comment shows the device without findmnt's `[subvol]` suffix; util-linux `findmnt` is no longer needed. recsetup no longer needs `recchroot` next to it.
  - **Bugs fixed:** recstrap `--check --json` reported `rootfs_bytes: 0` for a partition (metadata length); now the declared image size. The swap-path check now unescapes `\040`.
  - **Left:** TL065 (extraction still uses GNU `cp -aT`, so AcornOS keeps coreutils; replacing it needs an xattr/hardlink-preserving copier and a VM run) and TO1028 (recab stage still runs `recstrap` by argv) — both TO-01 follow-ups. TO2082 only half: recab/recsetup/recfstab share the lower-case PARTUUID rule, but recpart's JSON stays upper-case because disk-plan compares upper-case type GUIDs (G20/G21). TO1129 (delete the recuki binary) not done: it needs `xtask/src/tasks/generate.rs` `DOCUMENTED_TOOLS` and `docs/content/.../13-recuki.ts`, both being edited by other fixes. BR099's builder half, BR102, BR103: `distro/builder/src/recipe/tools.rs` still passes `RUST_TARGET` (now ignored) and keys the work dir on it, and still computes `INSTALL_PATH`; it was mid-edit by G05, so the lead or G05 drops them. Shipping the dual-boot resize tools on both is a package-set change in `distro.toml` (G08), not touched. `cargo xtask gen` must be re-run: recab, recinit and reciso `--help` changed (`docs/content/src/tool-help.ts`). The builder submodule pointer was not moved here (its HEAD also has G05/G22/G32 commits).
- [x] G31 — ginux-boot stages both OSes the same way and judges boot health through recab. Superproject commit "G31: …" (`testing/ginux-boot/**` only).
  - **Layout now:** `ginux-boot.sh` (driver), `stage.sh` (one staging), `probe.sh` (one probe), `instance.toml` (one instance), and per OS only `hook.sh` (how the init starts the probe) and `expect`. Gone: `acorn/{stage.sh,probe.sh,instance.toml}`, `levitate/{stage.sh,probe.sh,instance.toml,ginux-probe.service}`.
  - **Judge (TS-05):** the probe runs `recab health --json --timeout T` behind one `health()` function in `probe.sh`, writes the JSON to `/ginux-probe.json` and recab's stderr to `.err`. The driver checks the JSON's shape with `jq`; with no verdict it FAILs with recab's stderr and the console tail. Both OSes then go through one rule: `finished` must be true, and the `failed` set must equal the gap set. No `if acorn` branch, no awk over `rc-status`/`systemctl` columns, no inner `timeout 120`, no `2>/dev/null` on the probe.
  - **Staging (TS-06):** `stage.sh` holds Ginux's `prepare_root` fakes as of `ginux_rev`, the same for both OSes: `/proc`, `/sys`, the cgroup tree, and device nodes that are always recreated. It adds `/proc/filesystems` and a `/proc/meminfo` derived from `instance.toml`'s `memory_mib`, and keeps the nobody-identity chown with its reason stated. It installs `/usr/local/sbin/ginux-probe` (spelled once) and calls `<os>/hook.sh ROOT PROBE_COMMAND`:
    - AcornOS appends one `::once:` line to the product's own inittab;
    - LevitateOS writes the unit plus its `multi-user.target.wants` link, with no `[Install]`.
    
    Nothing calls Ginux's `ginux-host-instance-helper.sh` any more. There are no forced runlevel links, no hostname and no host timezone. One `instance.toml` for both OSes: `args = []`, `container=ginux`, `net0` and the shared network.
  - **Process (TS-07):** the probe powers off through the init, and Ginux turns PID 1's `reboot(POWER_OFF)` into the instance exiting. `boot()` runs `sudo timeout -k 10 (T+60) ginux native …` through `script -c` with every argument quoted by `quote()`, and reports how the instance ended (powered off, stopped by timeout, killed, exit status). An instance that does not power off is a FAIL.
    - Gone: `pkill`, the `sleep` poll, the `.tmp` rename, `|| true`, and the `keep=1` flips. The EXIT trap keeps the work dir exactly when the exit status is nonzero (or with `--keep`), and reports a failed removal.
    - `--timeout` must be a number. `/sbin/init` is resolved inside the rootfs (absolute targets relative to it).
    - Checked up front: `sudo script cargo git timeout mknod awk jq`.
    - The Ginux checkout must contain `ginux_rev=5cd55224…` (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`).
  - **Gaps (TS-08):** `read_gaps()` is the one parser for `expect`, whose format is stated at the top of each file; malformed lines are an error. A gap that no longer fails is a FAIL ("remove it from expect"), and the count comes from `set -- $gaps`, so an empty list gives 0. The README now copies nothing: no gap list, results table, Ginux commit/PR table or recipe file names. It says no run of this version is recorded yet.
  - **Verified:**
    - `sh -n` and `bash -n` on all five scripts.
    - `quote()` round-trips spaces, quotes, `;` and `$`.
    - `read_gaps()` handles both expect files, an empty file (count 0) and malformed lines (rc 1).
    - The `/sbin/init` resolution works on both live-tools `rootfs-source` trees.
    - The Ginux revision check passes for `~/Projects/ginux-boot` (`levitate/boot`) and refuses `ginux-pr235`.
    - A dry `stage.sh` run on fake roots, with `mknod` and `chown` stubbed, showed the appended inittab line, the unit and its link, the cgroup tree, and meminfo/cmdline.
    - The jq shape check accepts a valid verdict and refuses a null `failed` and an empty file.
    - **No Ginux run** (quiet hours).
  - **Behaviour changes (test-only):**
    - Every run FAILs until recab has `health` (G19).
    - The rootfs must be a live-tools `rootfs-source`, since that tree carries `/usr/bin/recab`. It also needs the G04 `REC_*` os-release lines; the current live-tools trees predate them, so they wait on the deferred rebuild.
    - AcornOS boots only its own runlevels (not `sshd`) and has a network.
    - LevitateOS boots its default target (`multi-user.target` in the product) instead of `--unit=multi-user.target`.
    - The success report no longer lists working services, because recab's verdict names only failures.
    - Stale gaps fail instead of printing a NOTE.
    - The host's `/etc/resolv.conf` is no longer copied in.
  - **Gap lists:** not re-recorded. In AcornOS `sshd` (no longer enabled) and `dhcpcd` (its cause was "no network") are dropped; LevitateOS's are kept. The first real run will rewrite both. Risk to watch on that run: the AcornOS product inittab's getty `respawn` lines for tty1–6/ttyS0 now run under Ginux, where the old hand-written inittab had none.
  - **Left:**
    - TS1021: `[instance] root` stays, because Ginux's schema requires it (bit 2 of `REQUIRED` in `ginux-instance`). The comment says so.
    - TS2034: the recorded pass needs a re-run from `rootfs-source` (daytime batch, after G19).
    - Re-recording the gaps, and the DECISION on booting the installed-system configuration (the default, the rootfs as given, is kept).
  - **Spec for G19 — `recab health --json [--timeout SECONDS]`.** It is read-only and needs no A/B layout or state. It runs on the booted system and reuses `running_profile()`'s init and `wait_for_startup(init, timeout)`; the default timeout is 900, like `trial`.
    - **stdout:** exactly one JSON object.
      ```json
      {"init": "systemd" | "openrc",
       "finished": true | false,
       "state": "<string>",
       "failed": ["<name>", ...]}
      ```
    - **`finished`:** true when startup ran to its end.
      - systemd: `is-system-running --wait` answered `running` or `degraded`.
      - OpenRC: softlevel `default` with `starting/` empty and no `openrc` process.
      - It is false for a timeout, `maintenance`, the single-user runlevel, `shutdown`/`reboot`/`stopping`, or no answer.
    - **`state`:** the init's own word: systemd's `is-system-running` answer, OpenRC's softlevel, or `timed out`.
    - **`failed`:** sorted, unique bare unit/service names: systemd's `list-units --failed` first column, or OpenRC's `openrc_failed`. Crashed daemons count as plain names, without the ` (crashed)` suffix; the trial's text may keep the suffix. It holds the failures even when `finished` is false.
    - **Exit status and errors:** exit 0 whenever a verdict is printed (healthy or not). Exit nonzero with a message on stderr and nothing on stdout when no verdict can be formed: a missing `REC_INIT`, or the init tool cannot be run.
    - **Callers:** `testing/ginux-boot/probe.sh` `health()` calls it, and `ginux-boot.sh` checks the four keys' types with jq.

- [x] G13 (recipe and guest half) — One installed-system policy and one live-system policy over a small init backend. builder `3363167`, superproject commit "G13: …" (builder pointer only). Paths: `recipes/{installed-system,live-overlay}.rhai`, `guest/live/*`, `guest/installed/system-setup.sh`, plus the one-line call site in each rootfs producer (`installed::apply(rootfs, inputs, "systemd"|"openrc")`). No `distro.toml` change.
  - **Backend (`installed-system.rhai`, keyed on the `init` string):**
    - `enable` reads one OpenRC runlevel table (`openrc_runlevel`). `base_services` and `network_services` keep each distro's current stack (G28 switches it).
    - `consoles(root, init, [[console, login]])`, where login is a prompt, `root` or a program. systemd gets one `getty.target.wants` link per console plus a per-instance agetty drop-in. OpenRC gets one busybox inittab, built once for live and installed.
    - Also `boot_target`, `locale_env`, `sshd_runtime_dir` and `shutdown_hook` (systemd: a unit with no `[Install]`; OpenRC: `local.d/*.stop` plus the `local` service).
    - `put`/`link`/`directory` always record modes through `target_mode`. `shadow_line` holds the aging fields once. `root_password` throws when there is no root line, on live too (RG1069).
  - **Installed policy (`apply`):** issue, `locale.conf`, sshd privsep dirs, a prompt on tty1–6 and ttyS0, boot target, base and network services checked present, locked root, empty machine-id, setup program. The `sshd_config.anaconda` fallback and the locale path probes are gone; the Fedora producer's readiness list already requires both files.
  - **Live policy (`live-overlay.rhai`, imports the module):**
    - The install screen runs on the listed consoles; root autologins on every other one of tty1–6/ttyS0, on both distros. Both accept the same console set.
    - Also: sysrq, tmpfs `/var/log` through `etc/fstab` (the same file on both inits; Fedora's `audit.conf` tmpfiles recreates `/var/log/audit`), the sshd runtime dir, services with no OpenRC whitelist, and shutdown cleanup on both.
    - No live-only network files: each distro's installed stack brings up every interface.
    - The machine-id is not rewritten (the base already has the empty one).
    - One strict `asset()` renderer: any `@Name@` left over, of any case, throws.
  - **Guest:**
    - Deleted `live-net-setup.sh` (NetworkManager autoconnects every wired device), `efivarfs.start` (OpenRC's `sysfs` mounts efivarfs, and `mdev` needs `sysfs`) and `serial-autologin.rootfs.sh` (unused; the serial profile prints the marker on every path).
    - `live-shutdown-cleanup.sh` lazily detaches `@LIVE_MEDIA_DIR@` and every loop device with a backing file, and reports failures. The dead `/rootfs`, `/live-overlay`, `/media/cdrom` and `loop0`/`loop1` literals are gone.
    - `shell-ux.sh` states the prompt once. `live-help` lists the current install flow and says what exit does on each console. The `system-setup.sh` header no longer restates recsetup facts.

    It also ran `installed::apply` on fake systemd and OpenRC trees. The trees it wrote look as intended. **No recipe or ISO run.**
  - **Behaviour changes:**
    - **AcornOS live:** tty2–6 autologin root; shutdown cleanup; `local` enabled. The QEMU-only `eth0` ifupdown stanza is gone, so dhcpcd alone handles every NIC, as on the installed system.
    - **LevitateOS live:**

    - Profile fragments are 0644. The shutdown unit now orders against the real `run-live\x2dmedia.mount`; the old `run-live-media.mount` named a unit that does not exist.
  - **Risk for the daytime run:** `live-boot.sh` on AcornOS checks for a default route at once, and dhcpcd (`-q -B`, backgrounded) no longer blocks boot the way `ifup eth0` did. The check should wait for the route (testing, G26).

  - **The pin:** `distro/builder/recipes/kernel/metadata.rhai` `pins()` is the one table: per variant (`KERNEL_TARGET`) its kernel line (`lts`/`latest`), the exact kernel.org version and the source sha256. `source_spec` derives the localversion (`-<id>`) and the exact release. `linux-prebuilt.rhai` keeps only what is the published asset's: its repo and digest (the asset name and URL are derived). `6.12.116`/`7.3.3` and the source digests now appear in that one file; `[build.kernel]` is gone from both `distro.toml` (the variant declares only `[profile] kernel_line`) and `BuildContract`/`KernelBuildContract` from the contract.
  - **One check:** `metadata.rhai` `release_mismatch` (exact `kernel.release` == pinned release) and `bundle_mismatch` (release, `staging/boot/vmlinuz`, `staging/usr/lib/modules/<release>`) are used by both recipes' readiness and by the prebuilt payload validation. distro-builder evaluates the same two functions (and `source_spec`) through the `rhai` crate with five file helpers, so `kernel::pin` and `kernel::status` read the recipe's own pin and rule without running Recipe or needing privileges. Gone: `starts_with` version matching in the recipes, xtask (`verify_one`) and `distro_contract::runtime` (which now checks only that the kernel files exist), and the `/lib/modules` branches.
  - **One owner:** `distro_builder::kernel` has `pin`, `status` (Ready/Missing/Mismatch with the reason), `install(variant, Source::Prebuilt | Source::Compile { autofix }, force)` and `check_kernel_installed`. Each builds its one Recipe run through G09's `recipe::Invocation` (so `--state-dir` and the process policy apply to kernel builds too), gives the recipe the merged kconfig and the resolved `current` bundle (a dangling `current` is an error naming it), grants the network hosts (`github.com`/`release-assets.githubusercontent.com` for prebuilt, `cdn.kernel.org` for source), and limits autofix to the recipes directory constant. `recipe/linux.rs` is only `select_kernel_bundle`; `linux_for_distro`, `LinuxPaths`, `kconfig_inputs`, `KERNEL_RECIPE` and `ensure_kernel_preinstalled` are gone (the release build calls `check_kernel_installed` and adds the `cargo xtask build kernel` hint itself, and no longer creates `.artifacts/kernel/<id>/current` before the check).
  - **xtask:** `tasks/kernels/` is `mod.rs` only: the `build kernel` arm over `kernel::install`, `status`/`fix` for `build <product>` and doctor, and the 23:00–10:00 window (local time from `localtime_r`, no `date`). `generate` takes the kernel version for `docs/content` from `kernel::pin` (same values). `--now` conflicts with `--prebuilt`; `--autofix-attempts` must be ≥ 1.
  - **The kconfig:** `distro/variants/_shared/kconfig` is the shared policy; `<id>/kconfig` holds only options of its kernel line (LevitateOS: `CRYPTO_SHA256/512_SSSE3`; AcornOS: `NETFILTER_XTABLES_LEGACY`); distro-builder gives the recipes the two concatenated as the one `kconfig` input (no `kconfig.d`). Neither sets `CONFIG_LOCALVERSION`: `linux.rhai` appends it from the pin and refuses a kconfig that sets it. The values are appended to the defconfig `.config` (a later assignment wins) instead of an unquoted `scripts/config` loop, and after `olddefconfig` **every `CONFIG_` line whose value the `.config` does not carry fails the build**, listed (`kernel::kconfig_dropped`). The prebuilt recipe runs the same comparison against a bundle's `kernel-build/.config`; a bundle without one passes with a warning (both published bundles today).
  - **D8, converged symbols (both kernels):** `PREEMPT=y` + `PREEMPT_DYNAMIC=y` (`PREEMPT_LAZY=n`), `HZ_1000`/`HZ=1000`, `NO_HZ_FULL=y`, `ZRAM=y` with `ZRAM_BACKEND_ZSTD=y` + `ZRAM_DEF_COMP_ZSTD=y`, `LIVEPATCH=y` (+ `FUNCTION_TRACER=y`, `DYNAMIC_FTRACE=y`, which it needs), `CHECKPOINT_RESTORE=y`, `BLK_DEV_INITRD=y`, `DEFAULT_HUNG_TASK_TIMEOUT=120`, `RAS=y` + `EDAC=y` + `EDAC_DECODE_MCE=m` + `EDAC_AMD64=m`, `LEDS_CLASS_MULTICOLOR=y` (for `HID_LOGITECH*`, `HID_PLAYSTATION`), `IP_NF_IPTABLES_LEGACY=y` (`IP_NF_NAT`, `IP_NF_TARGET_MASQUERADE`); off: `MODULE_FORCE_UNLOAD=n`, `IWLWIFI_DEBUG=n`, `SCHED_DEBUG=n`; dropped `INITRAMFS_COMPRESSION_GZIP` and the obsolete `EDAC_MM_EDAC`.
  - **The stale kconfig lines:** run against both kernel trees, ~48 (6.12) and ~60 (7.3) requested values never took effect. Renamed to the current name with the value they already resolved to: `X86_INTEL_PSTATE`, `X86_AMD_PSTATE`, `X86_AMD_PSTATE_DEFAULT_MODE`, `SCSI_VIRTIO`, `SND_CTL_LED`. Removed (no such option any more, or not settable): `MICROCODE_INTEL/AMD`, `DEVPTS_FS`, `BLK_MQ_PCI/VIRTIO`, `EFI_VARS`, `THUNDERBOLT(_NET)` (USB4 stays), `BACKLIGHT_GENERIC`, `SND_RAWMIDI`, `VHOST`, `DRM_I915_GVT`, `CRYPTO_CHACHA20/POLY1305_X86_64`, `SQUASHFS_DECOMP_MULTI`, `MVNETA`/`MVPP2` (ARM only), `SCHED_CLASS_EXT` (needs BTF debug info), `MODULE_COMPRESS_ZSTD` (would compress modules; recinit's module copy is untested with `.ko.zst`), `EDAC_SKYLAKE/ICELAKE/SAPPHIRE_RAPIDS`. Parked as comments under "NOT BUILT YET" with the dependency each lacks: Dell platform drivers, Surface aggregator, Intel SOF/AVS audio, AMD PS audio, `INTEL_VSC`, `MMC_REALTEK_PCI`, `VIRTIO_PMEM/MEM`, `EDAC_GHES`, `NFT_NAT`, `BT_MTKUART`, `DRM_DP_AUX_CHARDEV`.
  - **Recipe fixes:** exact release checks; the prebuilt recipe's five hooks are one readiness function, validates the payload once, swaps trees by rename, logs "Downloading" only when it downloads, and drops the dead ctx fields and `.tmp` removals; the source recipe extracts into `<source>.extract` and renames the tree in (a partly extracted tree is never taken as acquired), verifies the release before swapping the build in, swaps build and staging by rename, moves `lib/modules` with `mv` (no `|| true`), and installs with the pinned release instead of re-reading it.
  - **Verified:** the shared + per-line kconfig run through the new configure step (defconfig, append, `olddefconfig`) on the real 6.12.116 and 7.3.3 trees: no value dropped on either; the resolved diff against the published configs is the converged list above (plus AMD ACP audio, e.g. Steam Deck, which the old `scripts/config` loop had silently dropped). With the `rhai` crate the four recipe files compile; `source_spec` gives `6.12.116-levitate`/`7.3.3-acorn` and refuses an unknown target; `bundle_mismatch` accepts both installed `.artifacts/kernel/*/current` bundles; `kconfig_dropped` finds nothing in the new configs and flags 19 (LevitateOS) / 26 (AcornOS) values in the published ones. `cargo check -p distro-builder -p levitate-xtask -p install-tests --all-targets` passes on a tree of every repo's HEAD plus exactly these commits (the shared working tree does not compile tonight because of other agents' in-flight contract work). With that build, `doctor` reports `6.12.116-levitate` and `7.3.3-acorn` ready, a fake `6.12.1160-levitate` bundle as "mismatch: Wrong kernel.release …", a dangling `current` by name; `build kernel acorn --prebuilt` skips the installed kernel; `--now --prebuilt` is a usage error. Recipe's kernel e2e tests not run (privileged, ~27 min); traced by hand against `tests/e2e/kernel_{recipes,acquisition}.rs` (file names, `KERNEL_TARGET`, `kernel_archive`, `build_dir`, the acorn asset digest in `linux-prebuilt.rhai`, "Wrong kernel", "modules", "configuration changed", "unsafe kernel cleanup").
  - **Behaviour changes:** both kernels change scheduling and features (above) and need a rebuild and republish of both prebuilts (`kernel-prebuilt-publishing` steps); add `kernel-build/.config` to the runtime tarball (`package.sh`) so the config check runs on it. A kernel build fails when the kconfig asks for something the kernel drops. doctor and `build <product>` say "mismatch: <reason>" or "not built: <reason>" instead of a bare "not built". Kernel builds get `--state-dir` under the work dir (the first run re-checks acquisition) and the builder's process policy. The source build is now granted `cdn.kernel.org`.
  - **Left:** `selinux=0` in LevitateOS's live `kernel_cmdline` (PA-11 suggests `SECURITY_SELINUX=n` in the kconfig instead; not a differing option, left for the lead). The shared hook `build-release.sh` still spells `staging/usr/lib/modules` (C053, CV1041 → G16). Network hosts are the recipe URL's hosts spelled in Rust (Recipe has no way for a recipe to declare them). `date` in `base.rs`/`setup.rs` (X061's other two, not kernel). `CONTRACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` not bumped for the removed `[build.kernel]` (other agents own the schema version). Deleting it was refused by the permission classifier; the user needs to run `git -C distro/builder push origin --delete master`.

- [x] G09 — One host-command layer: one spawn helper, one Recipe runner, host prerequisites declared once. builder `e2eae2a`, superproject `bf4c27b` "G09: …" (pointer, xtask; CI's install step is in G27's ci.yml).
  - **One spawn helper:** `distro_builder::process::Cmd` is the only way the builder starts a program (tui_binary's bun, recipe::tools' cargo/git, pipeline::io's fsck.erofs, mkfs.erofs, Recipe, cargo for Recipe). Every child dies with the builder (`process::die_with_parent`, the one `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` in distro/xtask; main uses it too); exits are `exit code N` or `killed by SIGTERM (signal 15)` from one table; a spawn failure says "is not on PATH", "does not exist", "is not executable (permission denied)" or "is not a valid executable (exec format error)", and a missing working directory is named. PATH lookup is the `which` crate. Gone: `run`, `run_in`, `shell`, `shell_in`, `ensure_exists`, `find_first_existing`, `CommandResult`, the duplicate signal table, every `cfg(unix)` twin (process, recipe, outputs, linux, main), tui_binary's own `run`.
  - **One Recipe runner:** `recipe::binary(repo)` resolves once per process (`OnceLock`): `RECIPE_BIN` (must be a file), else `cargo build -q -p levitate-recipe` with the builder's own profile into `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` or `<repo>/target`. `recipe::Invocation::new(repo, phase, recipe, work)` with `.define/.input/.network_host/.memory_mib/.storage_max_objects/.force_acquire/.args` replaces `find_recipe`, `RecipeBinary`, `RecipeSource` and the three `run_recipe_phase_json_*` functions at every call site (overlay, kernel, linux, qemu, rootfs_source, tools, xtask kernels). `--recipes-path` is always `distro/builder/recipes`. The child loses every ambient `RECIPE_*`/`GINUX_*` variable except Recipe's documented launcher settings (`GINUX_BIN`, `GINUX_ROOT`, `GINUX_ROOTFS`, `RECIPE_WORKER_BIN`, `RECIPE_FRAME_TIMEOUT_SECS`), which are logged; its stdout goes to stderr. `RecipeResult` (`from_json`, `file`, `directory`, `optional_directory`, `metadata_string`) owns output lookup with one rule: a value is used exactly as reported, and one that is empty or has surrounding whitespace is rejected.
  - **Host prerequisites declared once:** `process::HOST_TOOLS` (22 programs: build, base lock, scenarios; dnf and apt packages, or an upstream for cargo/bun). `release build` runs `check_host_tools(Build)` before any work and lists every missing tool with its fix for the detected host; it also checks that mkfs.erofs has zstd and `--tar`. `cargo xtask base lock` checks `BaseLock` first. Doctor's tool rows (virsh, passt, qemu-img, ssh, ssh-keygen, bun, the ISO-tool row) are now one row per `HOST_TOOLS` entry with host-specific fixes; `cargo xtask doctor --packages <dnf|apt>` prints the package list (CI's apt line, now in G27's ci.yml, uses it). rootfs.rs prints no Fedora/Ubuntu/Arch hints.
  - **Verified:** the commit trees (builder + superproject, other submodules at their HEADs) checked in a separate worktree: `cargo check -p distro-builder -p levitate-xtask --all-targets` passes with no warnings. `doctor` lists every host tool ok on this host; `doctor --packages apt|dnf` print the lists; `--packages yum` is a usage error. `distro-builder release build iso live-boot levitate` with `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin` fails before any work, naming cargo, bun and mkfs.fat with fixes. No ISO build or Recipe run (quiet hours).
  - **Behaviour change:** a `recipe` on PATH and `RECIPE_SRC`/`RECIPE_BUILD_RELEASE` are no longer used; `preseed-rootfs-source --refresh` forces acquisition only, not reinstall (CL2068); a release build fails up front on a host missing any build tool (bun, fsck.erofs, readelf, xz/gzip/zstd, ukify, … now checked) or with erofs-utils < 1.8; doctor marks every build and scenario tool required (bun was optional) and drops the genisoimage/mkisofs fallback (xorriso is required); bun's and git's errors now carry only stderr; the builder's children (bun, cargo, git, sh) also die with it.
  - **Bugs fixed:** `which` spawn failure read as "program not found" (BC2073); signal deaths reported as "exit code -1" (CL2069); `" /x"` vs `"/x "` trim mismatch (CL3083); Recipe self-build progress on stdout (CL2072); the builder missing a Recipe built under `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (CL1072).

The entry prefix is read by the rule init writes it (RECAB_ENTRY_PREFIX, else the os-release ID): the default-entry and paired-prefix fallbacks are gone. recsetup's Bootloader and BootEntries sections are one `boot` step, `recab init …` on the host; recsetup has no `bootctl`, no entry/loader.conf text, no `Write`/`Copy` actions; `Target.bootloader`/`systemd_boot_efi` and `Step.content` left the plan JSON.
  - **Queries for the harness (TV-06):** `recab status --root <target> [--json]` reads an installed system (its root found by the PARTUUID of the partition mounted there). `Status.slots[]` gives per slot `entry` (ESP-relative), `boot_dir`, `root`, `device`, `bootable`, `trial`. `recab stage --from-media <device|dir>` mounts the medium read-only under `/run/recab/media`, stages `ROOTFS_ISO_PATH`, `KERNEL_ISO_PATH` and `INITRAMFS_INSTALLED_ISO_PATH` when present, and unmounts it.

The template spells none of these. Live init rewritten: medium found by `findfs LABEL=<label>` polled for `MEDIA_DEADLINE_SECS` (30 s; no device list, no fixed sleeps), injection medium found by its label too (`BOOT_DEVICE_PROBE_ORDER`, `BOOT_INJECTION_DEVICES` deleted), payloads only as files (`mount -t erofs` of the file; no losetup/direct-mount fallbacks), no two-layer special case, no `|| true`/`2>/dev/null` on mounts, chmods, mkdirs, injection; messages go to `/dev/kmsg` (every console), the shell to `/dev/console` (no forced ttyS0); `/sbin/init` must resolve inside the new root; the emergency text says what `exec sh` does. recinit refuses a busybox without the applets the init runs (`LIVE_INIT_APPLETS`). reciso has only the ISO-files layout (`--live-payload-layout` takes only `iso-files`, now the default; appended partitions deleted).   - **Trial service (TO-05):** recab's package carries the service enabled on both inits: `recab install-unit --package <init>` (what `install-tools.rhai` already runs) writes the unit/script, its enabling link (`sysinit.target.wants`, OpenRC `boot` runlevel) and the carried list, so every image (A/B, mutable, live) runs it, and `init`/`stage` no longer enable anything (TL087's "enabled on the running root" conditional and `enable_trial_service` are gone). `recab trial` returns at once when there is no recab state and the firmware shows no one-shot boot. The service files are templates filled from `RECAB_BIN` and `TRIAL_TIMEOUT_SECS` (900, also the CLI default); both pass `--timeout`. OpenRC's script records the trial's pid and its `stop()` ends the trial at shutdown (systemd's `Conflicts=shutdown.target`). Headers say "packaged by", not "installed by".
  - **Adapter:** `init_system.rs` is three operations: `wait_finished` (returns `Health`), `reboot`, `crash_option`. No unit text, no `include_str!`, no carry lists, no `trial_service_link` consumer inside recab (it moved to `package.rs` as the exported `recab::trial_service_link(init)`, the layout fact TV-06 wants). Trial options are one rule in `systemd_boot.rs`: `recinit::live::FAIL_REBOOT_OPTION` when the entry boots through an initramfs (an `initrd` line), plus the init's `crash_option`; stripping uses the same two sources (`ALL_TRIAL_OPTIONS`, `InitSystemExt::trial_options` deleted). OpenRC failures are bare names (no ` (crashed)` suffix), sorted and unique, the same format as systemd's.
  - **`recab health --json [--timeout S]` (G31's spec):** one JSON object `{"init","finished","state","failed"}` on stdout, exit 0 whenever a verdict is printed; no root, lock or A/B layout; a missing `REC_*` profile exits 20 with the message on stderr. `trial` judges from the same `Health`. Checked on this host: `{"init":"systemd","finished":true,"state":"running","failed":[]}`.
  - **Carried config (TO-06, decision):** the list is `usr/lib/recab/carried` in the image (packaged with recab from `src/services/carried`; the file states its format: `file`, `dir`, `prefix`, `accounts`), and `stage` reads the list of the image it writes, after extraction; an image without one fails the stage. `stage.rs` and `init_system.rs` hold no path lists. Contents: identity, locale and time files, the four account files (merged by name), SSH host keys (`prefix etc/ssh/ssh_host_`), `etc/systemd/system`, `etc/runlevels`, `etc/conf.d`, `etc/NetworkManager/system-connections`, `etc/wpa_supplicant`, `etc/network/interfaces`, `etc/sudoers.d`, `etc/doas.d`. A source that cannot be read, or a listed directory that is not one, fails the stage. Exported: `recab::carried::{read, Carry, CARRIED_LIST}`.
